Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

313: 	defaultGroup := t.GetDefaults().Group
314: 
315: 	nodeDefinition, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
316: 	if !ok {
317: 		return defaultGroup
318: 	}
319:

321: 		return nodeDefinition.Group
322: 	}

325:

328:

330: 			return kindGroup
331: 		}
332: 	}
333: 
334: 	return defaultGroup
335: }

339:

342: 		return defaultType
343: 	}
344:

346: 		return strings.TrimSpace(nodeDefinition.Type)

356: 		return strings.TrimSpace(kind.Type)
357: 	}
358: 
359: 	return defaultType
360: }

367: 		func(group *NodeDefinition) []string { return group.Ports },

371: 	)
372: }

374: func (t *Topology) GetNodePorts(nodeName string) (nat.PortSet, nat.PortMap, error) {
375: 	ports, bindings, _, err := t.GetNodePortMappings(nodeName)
376: 	return ports, bindings, err
377: }

379: // GetNodePortMappings parses the node's Docker and Tailscale port mappings together.
380: func (t *Topology) GetNodePortMappings(
381: 	nodeName string,
382: ) (nat.PortSet, nat.PortMap, []TailscalePort, error) {
383: 	docker, ts, err := SplitDockerAndTailscalePorts(t.nodePortSpecs(nodeName))
384: 	if err != nil {
385: 		return nil, nil, nil, err
386: 	}
387: 	if len(docker) > 0 {
388: 		ports, bindings, err := nat.ParsePortSpecs(docker)
389: 		return ports, bindings, ts, err
390: 	}
391: 
392: 	return nil, nil, ts, nil
393: }

Key review questions:

1. `getFieldPtr` change: previously `if nodeDefintion == nil || !ok { return finalDefault }`. Now: `if !ok { return finalDefault }` and then nil-check for node field. This changes behavior for nil node definitions: now they fall through to group/kind lookups. Good fix (that's the PR intent).

2. `GetKind` change: previously if kind not in map, did case-insensitive search and returned result; if exact match found with nil value (`kdef, ok := t.Kinds[kind]; ok` returns nil), it returned nil. Wait, base code: `if kdef, ok := t.Kinds[kind]; ok { return kdef }` — if kind present with nil value, returns nil. Now: if not ok, search; then if kdef == nil return new(NodeDefinition). So nil kind definitions now return empty definition instead of nil. But note: the `if t.Kinds == nil { return new(NodeDefinition) }` guard at top.

Wait, there's a subtlety: in the new code, if exact match exists but is nil (kdef = nil, ok = true), the loop is skipped (since ok is true), then kdef == nil → return new(NodeDefinition). Good.

But callers: `GetKind` previously could return nil; now returns non-nil always (when Kinds non-nil... actually returns new(NodeDefinition) when Kinds is nil too). Callers of GetKind: many places check `kind != nil`. Now the check `if kind != nil` never triggers since GetKind never returns nil. Hmm — is that a problem? Callers like:

```go
kind := topo.GetKind(topo.GetNodeKind(nodeName))
if kind != nil {
    fieldKind := getFieldKind(kind)
    ...
}
```

Now GetKind never returns nil, so the nil-check is dead but harmless. But is there a behavioral change? Previously, when kind was unknown, GetKind returned empty NodeDefinition (non-nil), so `getFieldKind(kind)` would be evaluated with zero value. Same now. When Kinds[kind] exists but nil, previously returned nil → skipped. Now returns empty → `getFieldKind` returns zero value → same result effectively. OK.

But wait — there may be callers that use the returned pointer identity or mutate it. E.g., code that does `t.GetKind(kind).Group` — fine. Let me search callers of GetKind to see if any relied on nil return (e.g., to detect unknown kinds). That's the key regression risk: previously `GetKind` returning nil could distinguish "kind defined without body"? Actually before this PR, unknown kind returned new(NodeDefinition) (non-nil), and nil-body kind returned nil. So callers using nil to detect something would be for nil-body kinds. Now both return empty. Let me grep for GetKind callers.

3. `GetNodeKind` change: `t.GetGroup(t.Defaults.Group)` → `t.GetGroup(t.GetDefaults().Group)`. Previously, if `t.Defaults` was nil, `t.Defaults.Group` would panic... wait, but line 286 already calls `t.GetDefaults().Kind`, so Defaults nil was already dereferenced safely. Hmm, base code: let me check base. The diff shows `defaults := t.GetGroup(t.Defaults.Group)` → `t.GetGroup(t.GetDefaults().Group)`. In base, if `t.Defaults` was nil, line 286 `t.GetDefaults().Kind` would be fine, but line 304 `t.Defaults.Group` would panic with nil pointer dereference. Actually wait — `t.Defaults.Group` where t.Defaults is nil *NodeDefinition: accessing field on nil pointer panics. So the new code fixes a potential panic. Good fix.

4. `GetNodeGroup`: similar nil-handling changes. Previously `if nodeDefinition == nil || !ok { return defaultGroup }` — so a nil node definition returned defaultGroup without consulting kind's group. Now nil node falls through to kind group lookup. But wait — `GetNodeKind` for a nil node: `nodeDefinition` is nil, ok true; falls to `defaultKind := t.GetDefaults().Kind`. Hmm wait, for nil node, GetNodeKind now: `!ok` false; `nodeDefinition != nil` false → skip; `nodeDefinition != nil` false → skip group; then `defaults := t.GetGroup(t.GetDefaults().Group)`... then return defaultKind. OK.

But GetNodeGroup for nil node: kind := t.GetNodeKind(nodeName) → returns defaultKind. Then `kindGroup := t.GetKind(kind).Group` — GetKind with empty kind "" → `t.Kinds[""]` not found → loop → not found → returns new(NodeDefinition) (since Kinds non-nil) or new... Then Group "" → falls to defaultGroup. Fine.

In the test: node1 nil, Defaults.Kind = "linux", Kinds["linux"].Group = "grp1". GetNodeGroup("node1"): nodeDefinition nil, ok true → falls through. kind := GetNodeKind("node1") → defaultKind = "linux" (node nil, group nil, defaults group nil → return defaultKind "linux"). GetKind("linux").Group = "grp1" → returns "grp1". Test expects "grp1". Good.

846: func (t *Topology) GetNodeBinds(nodeName string) ([]string, error) {
847: 	bindSources := mergeStringSliceFields(
848: 		t,
849: 		nodeName,
850: 		func(node *NodeDefinition) []string { return node.Binds },
851: 		func(group *NodeDefinition) []string { return group.Binds },
852: 		func(kind *NodeDefinition) []string { return kind.Binds },
853: 		func(defaults *NodeDefinition) []string { return defaults.Binds },
854: 	)
855: 
856: 	if len(bindSources) == 0 {
857: 		return nil, nil
858: 	}
859: 
860: 	binds := map[string]*Bind{}
861: 
862: 	// add the binds from less to more specific levels, indexed by the destination path.
863: 	// thereby more specific binds will overwrite less specific one
864: 	for _, bind := range bindSources {
865: 		b, err := NewBindFromString(bind)
866: 		if err != nil {
867: 			return nil, err
868: 		}
869: 
870: 		binds[b.Dst()] = b
871: 	}
872: 
873: 	// in order to return nil instead of empty array when no binds are defined
874: 	if len(binds) == 0 {
875: 		return nil, nil
876: 	}
877: 
878: 	result := make([]string, 0, len(binds))
879: 
880: 	for _, b := range binds {
881: 		result = append(result, b.String())
882: 	}
883: 
884: 	slices.Sort(result)
885: 
886: 	return result, nil
887: }

1045: // credentialsPresent reports whether a credentials block supplies a username/password pair. The
1046: // identity-file is resolved on its own precedence chain (resolveTopologyIdentityFile) and is
1047: // intentionally excluded here so it does not influence the username/password winning level used by
1048: // inventory generation.
1049: func credentialsPresent(c NodeCredentials) bool {
1050: 	return c.Username != "" || c.Password != ""
1051: }
1052: 
1053: // resolveTopologyCredentials returns username and password from the most specific topology level
1054: // where either field is set (node, then group, then kind, then defaults). Values are not merged
1055: // across levels.
1056: func (t *Topology) resolveTopologyCredentials(
1057: 	nodeName string,
1058: ) (username, password string, src CredentialTopologySource) {
1059: 	nodeDefinition, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1060: 	if !ok {
1061: 		return "", "", CredentialTopologyUnset
1062: 	}
1063: 
1064: 	if nodeDefinition != nil && credentialsPresent(nodeDefinition.Credentials) {
1065: 		c := nodeDefinition.Credentials
1066: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyNode
1067: 	}
1068: 
1069: 	group := t.GetGroup(t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName))
1070: 	if group != nil && credentialsPresent(group.Credentials) {
1071: 		c := group.Credentials
1072: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyGroup
1073: 	}
1074: 
1075: 	kind := t.GetKind(t.GetNodeKind(nodeName))
1076: 	if kind != nil && credentialsPresent(kind.Credentials) {
1077: 		c := kind.Credentials
1078: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyKind
1079: 	}
1080: 
1081: 	defaults := t.GetDefaults()
1082: 	if defaults != nil && credentialsPresent(defaults.Credentials) {
1083: 		c := defaults.Credentials
1084: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyDefaults
1085: 	}
1086: 
1087: 	return "", "", CredentialTopologyUnset
1088: }
1089: 
1090: // resolveTopologyIdentityFile returns the SSH identity-file path from the most specific topology
1091: // level where it is set (node, then group, then kind, then defaults). It is resolved independently
1092: // of username/password so a node may set only identity-file and still have it apply, without
1093: // affecting the username/password winning level (see resolveTopologyCredentials).
1094: func (t *Topology) resolveTopologyIdentityFile(nodeName string) string {
1095: 	nodeDefinition, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1096: 	if !ok {
1097: 		return ""
1098: 	}
1099: 
1100: 	if nodeDefinition != nil && nodeDefinition.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1101: 		return nodeDefinition.Credentials.IdentityFile
1102: 	}
1103: 
1104: 	if group := t.GetGroup(t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName)); group != nil &&
1105: 		group.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1106: 		return group.Credentials.IdentityFile
1107: 	}
1108: 
1109: 	if kind := t.GetKind(t.GetNodeKind(nodeName)); kind != nil &&
1110: 		kind.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1111: 		return kind.Credentials.IdentityFile
1112: 	}
1113: 
1114: 	if defaults := t.GetDefaults(); defaults != nil && defaults.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1115: 		return defaults.Credentials.IdentityFile
1116: 	}
1117: 
1118: 	return ""
1119: }

1177: // KindSpecificConfigEntry is a raw kind-specific config key of a node with the topology block it
1178: // came from.
1179: type KindSpecificConfigEntry struct {
1180: 	Key   string
1181: 	Value any
1182: 	// From is the block that set the key: nodes.<name>, groups.<name>, kinds.<name> or defaults.
1183: 	From string
1184: }
1185: 
1186: // GetNodeKindSpecificConfig returns the node's raw kind-specific config keys merged with precedence
1187: // node > group > kind > defaults, sorted by key. The first block that sets a key provides its
1188: // whole value. Nodes absent from the topology have no kind-specific config.
1189: func (t *Topology) GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName string) []KindSpecificConfigEntry {
1190: 	nodeDef, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1191: 	if !ok {
1192: 		return nil
1193: 	}
1194: 
1195: 	group := t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName)
1196: 	kind := t.GetNodeKind(nodeName)
1197: 
1198: 	blocks := []struct {
1199: 		from string
1200: 		def  *NodeDefinition
1201: 	}{
1202: 		{"nodes." + nodeName, nodeDef},
1203: 		{"groups." + group, t.GetGroup(group)},
1204: 		{"kinds." + kind, t.GetKind(kind)},
1205: 		{"defaults", t.GetDefaults()},
1206: 	}
1207: 
1208: 	var entries []KindSpecificConfigEntry
1209: 
1210: 	seen := map[string]bool{}
1211: 
1212: 	for _, b := range blocks {
1213: 		if b.def == nil {
1214: 			continue
1215: 		}
1216: 
1217: 		for k, v := range b.def.KindSpecificConfig {
1218: 			if seen[k] {
1219: 				continue
1220: 			}
1221: 
1222: 			seen[k] = true
1223: 
1224: 			entries = append(entries, KindSpecificConfigEntry{Key: k, Value: v, From: b.from})
1225: 		}
1226: 	}
1227: 
1228: 	slices.SortFunc(
1229: 		entries,
1230: 		func(a, b KindSpecificConfigEntry) int { return strings.Compare(a.Key, b.Key) },
1231: 	)
1232: 
1233: 	return entries
1234: }

`new(expr)` with a value is used extensively in the existing codebase (e.g., `new(MgmtNet)`, `new(true)` in existing tests at line 21, 28, etc.), so the Go toolchain supports it (Go 1.26 feature). Not a finding.

Now let me check the base version of the changed hunks to confirm behavior changes, then check callers of GetKind / GetKinds / GetNodeGroup / GetNodeKind / GetNodeConfigDispatcher elsewhere in the codebase for nil-related regressions.

Key potential issues:

1. `GetNodeAliases` (line 1036-1043) still uses `if nodeDefinition == nil || !ok { return nil }` — pre-existing, unchanged. Not introduced by PR, so per rules don't flag (only mention if materially worsened — it's not).

2. `ImportEnvs` (line 993-1007): iterates `t.Kinds`, `t.Groups`, `t.Nodes` and calls `n.ImportEnvs()` on each — for nil entries this would panic (method on nil pointer: `ImportEnvs` likely has nil receiver handling? Let me check). If it's `func (n *NodeDefinition) ImportEnvs()` with nil check... need to check. This is pre-existing though — nil entries in Nodes/Kinds/Groups already existed before this PR (YAML `node1:` yields nil). So not introduced.

3. `GetKind` semantic change: previously a nil-body kind returned `nil` from GetKind, and callers like `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig` (line 1204) pass `t.GetKind(kind)` into blocks and skip nil. Now GetKind never returns nil (when Kinds non-nil), so `{"kinds." + kind, t.GetKind(kind)}` is non-nil empty def with no KindSpecificConfig — loop iterates empty map, no entries. Same result. Fine.

But wait — is there a caller that relied on GetKind returning nil to detect "kind not defined"? Let me grep for GetKind callers across the repo.

4. The `GetKind` change interacts with `GetNodeGroup` in the nil-kind test: `GetKind("linux")` where Kinds["linux"] = nil → returns new(NodeDefinition) with Group "" → GetNodeGroup returns defaultGroup "". Test expects "". Good.

5. Another subtle behavior: in `GetKind`, when `t.Kinds` is non-nil but the exact-match lookup hit with nil value, the case-insensitive loop is skipped. Fine since exact match takes precedence.

6. `GetNodeConfigDispatcher` — `if _, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]; !ok { return nil }` — for a nil node now proceeds; vars from defaults.Config.GetVars(), kind.Config, group.Config. Check `GetVars()` nil receiver safety. Let me check `ConfigDispatcher.GetVars` and `NodeDefinition.ImportEnvs`.

Also — one more: the changed `GetNodeKind` uses `t.GetDefaults().Group` for the defaults-group lookup. Previously `t.GetGroup(t.Defaults.Group)` — with t.Defaults non-nil but... the same semantics when Defaults non-nil. With nil Defaults, previously panic; now fixed. But note: line 286 `defaultKind := t.GetDefaults().Kind` already calls GetDefaults, so the PR change makes it consistent. Good.

Now check `GetVars`, `ImportEnvs`, and also search for other `== nil || !ok` patterns in the repo that reference Nodes, to see whether the PR addressed all sibling callers (ponytail rule: fix shared function once; patching only the path the ticket names leaves sibling callers broken). `GetNodeAliases` at line 1036 still has the old pattern — returns nil for nil node, which is arguably fine (nil node has no aliases; there's no group/kind-level alias inheritance... actually is there? Aliases exist only per node). Actually for aliases, inheritance from group/kind probably doesn't exist, so returning nil for nil node is correct behavior. Not a bug.

Let me now grep for `GetKind(`, `GetKinds(` callers outside types package, and check the base file hunks. Also check `GetVars` implementation.

step-start

  "type": "step-start",

Reasoning
Now let me trace all callers and potential regressions carefully.

### `GetKind` change analysis

Base behavior:
- `Kinds == nil` → `new(NodeDefinition)`
- exact match (even nil) → return kdef (could be nil)
- case-insensitive match → return kdef (could be nil)
- else → `new(NodeDefinition)`

Head behavior:
- `Kinds == nil` → `new(NodeDefinition)`
- exact match: kdef (maybe nil)
- if !ok, case-insensitive: kdef = t.Kinds[name] (maybe nil)
- if kdef == nil → `new(NodeDefinition)`
- return kdef

Change: nil-body kinds now return an empty `*NodeDefinition` instead of `nil`. Callers of GetKind:

- topology.go internal: all guard with `kind != nil`, which is now always true (dead check, harmless). Effect: for nil-body kind, previously skipped; now evaluate getFieldKind(empty def) → zero value → isSet false → same result. For `GetNodeConfigDispatcher`: `kind.Config != nil` — empty def's Config is nil → skip. Same. For `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig`: `t.GetKind(kind)` non-nil with nil KindSpecificConfig map → no entries. Same result. For `resolveTopologyCredentials`: `credentialsPresent(kind.Credentials)` on empty → false. Same.
- core/inventory.go:258, 466: `kindDef := topo.GetKind(...)` then `kindDef.Credentials.Username` — previously could panic if the kind was defined with a nil body (nil pointer dereference on `kindDef.Credentials`)! Actually wait — base GetKind returns kdef which could be nil for nil-body kinds, and inventory.go dereferences `kindDef.Credentials` unconditionally → panic. So this PR fixes a potential panic there too. Good.
- labruntime/clabernetes/manifests.go:104 — `copied.GetKind()` — different type, not related.

So the GetKind change is a strict improvement and compatible.

One more subtle point about GetKind: in the head version, when exact match exists with nil value (`ok=true, kdef=nil`), the case-insensitive loop is skipped, then nil → return empty. Behavior fine.

### `getFieldPtr` change

Base: nil node or missing → return finalDefault (no group/kind inheritance). Head: nil node falls through to group and kind. This matches `getField`, `mergeStringSliceFields`, `mergeStringMapFields` which already handled nil nodes and fell through. Consistent. Good.

### `GetNodeKind` change

`defaults := t.GetGroup(t.Defaults.Group)` → `t.GetGroup(t.GetDefaults().Group)`. Fixes potential nil-pointer panic when `t.Defaults` is nil (e.g., a topology constructed as `&Topology{}` with node present but no Defaults). Wait, but line 278 already calls `t.GetDefaults().Kind`, so for nil Defaults with a node present: base would panic at line 296. E.g., test `TestGetNodeFieldsOfNilKindAndDefaults` has topology with no Defaults and node1/node2 — `GetNodeKind("node2")` in base would panic at `t.Defaults.Group` (nil deref). Actually `t.Defaults` is nil → `t.Defaults.Group` panics. So the head change fixes this. Good.

### `GetNodeGroup` change

Base: `if nodeDefinition == nil || !ok { return defaultGroup }` — nil nodes did not inherit kind group. Head: nil node falls to `kind := t.GetNodeKind(nodeName)` and kind group. This is the fix tested by "group": expects "grp1" via kind's Group.

Hmm, wait — is there a consistency question? `GetNodeKind` for a nil node: checks `nodeDefinition != nil` for group kind lookup — so a nil node can't get a kind via its group. But kind group inheritance via defaults.Kind works. In the test, Defaults.Kind = "linux" → GetNodeKind returns "linux" → GetKind("linux").Group = "grp1". Good.

### `GetNodeConfigDispatcher` change

Base: nil node → return nil (no dispatcher). Head: proceeds; vars from defaults, kind, group; node nil so no node vars. Returns `&ConfigDispatcher{Vars: merged}`. The test expects `&ConfigDispatcher{Vars: map[string]any{"var1": "kind"}}` for node1 (nil node with kind config vars).

Now: `GetDefaults().Config.GetVars()` — `GetDefaults()` returns non-nil; `Config` may be nil → `GetVars()` nil-safe (checked: returns nil if cd nil). `clabutils.MergeMaps(vars, nil)` — need to check MergeMaps handles nil second arg. Let me check utils.MergeMaps.

Also check callers of `GetNodeConfigDispatcher` that might rely on nil return for missing/nil nodes. In base, a nil node (`node1:` with no body) returned nil from GetNodeConfigDispatcher; now returns non-nil with vars merged from kind/group/defaults. If some caller does `if cd := topo.GetNodeConfigDispatcher(name); cd != nil { ... }` and treats non-nil as "has config" — behavior change, but the merged vars is what would be used anyway. Let me check callers.

Also, one potential issue: in head GetNodeConfigDispatcher, when there are no vars at all (all nil), `MergeMaps(nil, nil)` — what does it return? If it returns nil, then `ConfigDispatcher{Vars: nil}` vs base for a present node... For present non-nil nodes behavior unchanged. For nil nodes, previously returned nil, now possibly `&ConfigDispatcher{Vars: nil}`. Any callers distinguishing? Let me check.

Also `GetStages`, `GetCertificateConfig` — unchanged, already nil-safe.

Now check `MergeMaps`:

Reasoning
`MergeMaps` handles nils fine. The `GetNodeConfigDispatcher` caller in core/config.go assigns result to `nodeCfg.Config`; downstream code (config exec) presumably checks for nil or uses `Config.Vars`. With a nil node, base returned nil → downstream skipped config; head returns non-nil with merged vars (possibly empty map from MergeMaps, which always returns non-nil). Downstream: let's check where `nodeCfg.Config` is used — e.g., in config.go "config" deploy step: `if node.Config != nil` or `len(node.Config.Vars)`. Behavior change: for a nil-body node, previously no ConfigDispatcher → no template vars; now an empty dispatcher with kind/defaults vars. That's actually the intended fix (nil nodes should inherit). Let me check how nodeCfg.Config is consumed to ensure empty Vars doesn't break anything (e.g., templating with nil vars).

Reasoning
The interesting part: in core, nodes are usually built from topology via `createNodeCfg` iterating `topo.Nodes`, so a nil `*NodeDefinition` in the topology map would be passed to `createNodeCfg(name, nodeDef, idx)`. Let me check how node definitions are iterated in core to see whether nil node definitions cause a panic elsewhere — e.g., `core/labruntime.go` line 495 or wherever nodes are constructed. If node creation dereferences `nDef.Kind` etc., a nil definition panics before even reaching GetNode* helpers. But that's pre-existing (nil entries existed before). The PR fixes the Get* helpers only. Let me check where nodes are created from topology: probably `core/labruntime.go` or `core/nodes.go`. Let me search `for ... range topo.Nodes` / `topo.GetNodes` maybe. Also there may be a `Sanitize`/normalization step that replaces nil definitions. Let me check `topology Sanitize` or `ParseTopology`.

5. `GetNodeAutoRemove`: group new(true) → true. ✓.

Result &ConfigDispatcher{Vars: {"var1":"kind"}}. ✓.

**TestGetNodeFieldsOfNilKindAndDefaults** topology:
- Kinds: {"linux": nil}
- Nodes: {"node1": {Kind: "linux"}, "node2": nil}

1. `GetKind("linux")`: Kinds non-nil; exact match kdef=nil, ok=true; loop skipped; kdef==nil → return new(NodeDefinition). want &NodeDefinition{} ✓.

2. `GetNodeGroup("node1")`: node non-nil, Group "" → skip; kind := GetNodeKind("node1"): defaultKind = "" (Defaults nil → GetDefaults empty); nodeDefinition.Kind = "linux" → return "linux". GetKind("linux") → empty def → Group "" → not ≠ "" → return defaultGroup "". want "" ✓.

3. `GetNodeKind("node2")`: node2 nil → ok; nodeDefinition nil checks skipped; defaults := GetGroup(GetDefaults().Group = "") → nil; return defaultKind "". want "" ✓. (In base this would panic at `t.Defaults.Group`.)

4. `GetNodeConfigDispatcher("node1")`: node exists. vars = GetDefaults().Config.GetVars() → nil. kind = GetKind(GetNodeKind("node1") = "linux") → empty def, Config nil → skip. group = GetGroup(GetNodeGroup("node1") = "") → nil → skip. nodeDefinition Config nil → skip. Return &ConfigDispatcher{Vars: nil}? Wait — MergeMaps never called since kind.Config nil, group nil. vars stays nil. So result is &ConfigDispatcher{Vars: nil}. want: &ConfigDispatcher{} — Vars is nil in that too. cmp.Diff equal ✓.

Now, consider cmp.Diff on `want: &ConfigDispatcher{}, got: &ConfigDispatcher{Vars: nil}` — equal. ✓.

Everything checks out. Now let's think about issues to report.

**Potential issue 1: Inconsistent nil-node handling remains in `GetNodeAliases` (head line 1036-1043).** Pre-existing; uses `nodeDefinition == nil || !ok` → returns nil for nil nodes. But aliases are node-only (no group/kind inheritance), so returning nil is correct; not inconsistent in a harmful way. Not a finding (pre-existing, unchanged).

**Potential issue 2: Dead nil-checks on `GetKind` result.** Since `GetKind` never returns nil now (all paths return non-nil), the `kind != nil` checks at lines 49, 104, 145, 355(?), 942, 973, 1019, 1076, 1109 are dead. Also `t.GetKind(kind).Group` at 327 — fine. These dead checks are pre-existing patterns (they were already partially dead in base for unknown kinds). The PR makes them fully dead. Is this worth a P4? Karpathy says surgical changes; the PR author didn't clean them up — arguably consistent with "don't improve adjacent code". The dead checks are harmless and defensive. I might mention as P4 or skip. Hmm. Actually they serve as defense if GetKind is later changed. I'd note as coverage note or a P4 at most. Given "one finding per root cause" and non-inflation, I'll mention in coverage notes rather than a finding. Actually, wait — the PR itself added new code in GetNodeConfigDispatcher: `if kind != nil && kind.Config != nil` — unchanged from base (it kept the nil check). Not newly added.

**Potential issue 3: `GetKinds()` returns the raw map with nil entries.** Pre-existing. Callers? Only used in topology.go itself... grep showed only definition. Actually let me check other packages for GetKinds usage — grep showed only types/topology.go:257. It's dead-ish. Not this PR.

**Potential issue 4: `GetKind` O(n log n) sorted scan per miss.** Pre-existing pattern (base had identical loop). Head only restructured. Performance: for each node field lookup with a kind that misses exact match, it sorts all kind names. Base had same behavior. Not worsened. Skip.

**Potential issue 5: The `if kdef == nil` check placement.** If exact match is nil AND a case-insensitive different-cased key exists with non-nil value — head: since ok=true for the exact key, the loop is skipped, so the differently-cased sibling is ignored, returns empty. Base: same (returned nil immediately). Consistent precedence. Good.

**Potential issue 6: Test table eager evaluation.** `got` evaluated at composite-literal construction time; all are pure lookups, no error states. Fine; but if any panics, the panic occurs outside t.Run and fails the test with a stack trace — acceptable.

**Potential issue 7: `getNodeVolumeSources` reused for group and kind** — pre-existing oddity, unrelated.

**Potential issue 8: comment accuracy in GetKind.** "unknown kinds and kinds defined without a body have no definition" — accurate.

**Potential issue 9: Does anything rely on `GetNodeConfigDispatcher` returning nil for nil-body nodes** (e.g., to skip config steps)? Grep showed the only assignment is core/config.go:466 into NodeConfig.Config, and nothing reads `.Config.Vars` from NodeConfig in Go code found... Actually the config machinery probably accesses via `nodeCfg.Config` in templates? Let me search more thoroughly — the "config" deploy step uses `clabexec` etc. Let me grep for `Config.Vars` more broadly and `Vars` in core/config.go.

Reasoning
OK, `NodeConfig.Config`'s runtime consumption isn't obvious from greps; the config dispatcher in containerlab is used by external consumers (e.g., the config engine reads `NodeConfig.Config.Vars` for templating in the "config" package which may have been removed, or clabernetes). Either way, the PR's change makes the returned value richer/more correct — inheriting kind/defaults vars for bodyless nodes, which matches the intent tested.

Let me now double-check one more potential inconsistency introduced: `GetNodeGroup` for nil node now can return the kind's group. But `GetNodeKind` for a nil node can return the defaults' group's Kind. Consider: defaults.Group = "g1", groups.g1.Kind = "srl", kinds.srl.Group = "g2". For nil node: GetNodeGroup → GetNodeKind returns "srl" (from defaults group) → GetKind("srl").Group = "g2" → returns "g2". Hmm — is that desirable? For a non-nil node with no group: GetNodeKind: node.Kind empty → node's group nil → defaults group kind "srl" → same → GetNodeGroup returns "g2". So consistent with the non-nil-but-empty node path. Fine.

Edge: recursion between GetNodeGroup and GetNodeKind? GetNodeKind does not call GetNodeGroup (it uses nodeDefinition.Group directly and defaults group). No cycle.

Now, schema check: no topology API change — internal fix only. clab.schema.json unchanged; nothing to check both directions really. YAML `nodes: {node1: }` producing nil is within schema (node can be null? Actually schema might require node objects; but YAML short form `sw01:` with null is common in the wild; the schema may allow null. Not needed).

Docs: no user-visible doc change needed; this is a bug fix. Possibly a release note? The repo has docs/rn. Not required.

Now let me also check `core/labruntime.go:495` and other places where nil node defs might now matter — no change.

Now the one thing I want to double check: in `GetKind`, when `t.Kinds` is non-nil and kind has exact nil entry — head returns `new(NodeDefinition)`. But there's a subtle case: `t.Kinds` contains exact key with nil value AND there's a case-variant key with a real def. Base returned nil (→ callers skip kind-level). Head returns empty def (→ callers read zero fields — same as skipping). Equivalent results.

Another check: `GetKind` when `t.Kinds == nil` returns empty def — unchanged.

Now, dead-code consideration: `GetKind` can no longer return nil, so all `kind != nil` guards are dead. Also in `getField`/`getFieldPtr`/`merge*` the comment "if the node definition is not nil" — fine.

Reasoning
`ImportEnvs` at line 994: `t.Defaults.ImportEnvs()` — nil-safe because `ImportEnvs` checks `n == nil` (line 211). A nil receiver method call with a nil-check inside is fine. OK.

Now, let me review the diff hunk for `GetKind` once more for a subtle bug: 

```go
kdef, ok := t.Kinds[kind]
if !ok {
    for _, name := range slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(t.Kinds)) {
        if strings.EqualFold(name, kind) {
            kdef = t.Kinds[name]
            break
        }
    }
}

// unknown kinds and kinds defined without a body have no definition
if kdef == nil {
    return new(NodeDefinition)
}

return kdef
```

Wait — one subtle issue: if exact match `ok=true` with `kdef=nil` (nil-body kind), we skip the loop. Then kdef==nil → empty def. But what if a case-insensitive variant with a real definition exists? E.g., Kinds = {"cisco_iol": real, "Cisco_IOL": nil}, GetKind("Cisco_IOL") → exact nil → returns empty def, ignoring the real "cisco_iol". Base behaved the same (returned nil). Precedence preserved. Fine.

But another subtle case: exact match nil with ok=true, and NO other variants — returns empty. Fine.

What about: exact match missing (!ok), loop finds variant with nil value → kdef = nil → returns empty. Fine.

OK. Now let me think about whether `GetKind` returning an empty definition could cause a *behavioral regression* anywhere that previously got nil and did something different from "empty". Scan callers:

- topology.go:48 getField: `kind != nil` → now always true. Previously nil only when kind body nil → getFieldKind(nil) would panic if evaluated... in base, nil → skip. Now: empty def → getFieldKind returns zero → isSet(zero) false → skip. Same outcome.
- 103 getFieldPtr: same.
- 144/185 merge*: same.
- 327 GetNodeGroup: `t.GetKind(kind).Group` — no nil check even in base! Base: if kind body nil → GetKind returns nil → `.Group` on nil pointer → panic! Wait: `t.GetKind(kind).Group` — if GetKind returns nil, field access on nil pointer panics. In base, this was reachable when the node's kind was defined with a nil body (`kinds: linux:`) and node had no group. So base had a latent panic here that head fixes (GetKind now never returns nil). 
- 354 GetNodeType: nil-guarded, same.
- 941 GetNodeConfigDispatcher: nil-guarded, same.
- 972 GetStages: nil-guarded.
- 1018 GetCertificateConfig: nil-guarded, `cc.Merge(kind.Certificate)` — Merge presumably nil-safe.
- 1075 resolveTopologyCredentials: nil-guarded.
- 1109: nil-guarded.
- 1204 GetNodeKindSpecificConfig: block def nil → skipped; now empty → no KindSpecificConfig keys → same entries.
- core/inventory.go:258,466: dereferences `kindDef.Credentials` unconditionally. Base: panic if kind body nil. Head: fixed.

So the GetKind change also fixes latent panics. 

Now the review findings. What's actually worth reporting?

Candidate findings:

1. **Dead nil-checks after `GetKind`** — `GetKind` can no longer return `nil`, so guards like `if kind != nil` (topology.go:49, 104, 145, 185, 355, 942, 973, 1019, 1076, 1109) are now dead code. This is pre-existing style made fully dead by this PR. A P4 cleanup suggestion: either keep as defense or simplify. Given the "surgical changes" rule, the PR didn't need to remove them, but since the PR changed `GetKind`'s contract (documented: never returns nil), a note is reasonable as P4. Hmm — is it actionable? It could mislead future maintainers into thinking GetKind may return nil. I'd rate P4.

2. **`getNodeVolumeSources` for group/kind** — unrelated pre-existing.

3. Anything about performance? `GetKind`'s sorted scan — pre-existing.

4. Test quality: the two new tests are good, assert-based, minimal per ponytail. One nit: table `got` values are computed eagerly before subtests — fine for pure functions.

5. Documentation: The behavior (bodyless nodes/kinds inherit) isn't documented anywhere user-visible, but it's a bug fix matching documented inheritance semantics (docs/manual/topo-def-file.md describes inheritance node > group > kind > defaults). The fix aligns implementation with documented behavior. So no docs gap. Maybe worth noting in coverage notes.

6. One more check — `GetNodeGroup` head for a node whose definition is non-nil but has empty Group, and kind has no group: falls to `defaultGroup`. Base identical. ✓.

7. Now, is there any place still using the old `== nil || !ok` pattern where nil now behaves inconsistently with the PR's new semantics, i.e., a bodyless node gets different inheritance in one getter vs another? `GetNodeAliases` (nil → nil). Aliases have no kind/group level, so consistent. Anything else? Let me grep for `== nil || !ok` across types:
